Standard numpy/pandas missing value\n- Empty strings `''` - May need explicit handling\n- Placeholder values like `'N/A'`, `'NA'`, `'-'`\n\n```python\n# Replace common placeholders with NaN before dropping\ndf = df.replace(['', 'N/A', 'NA', '-', 'null', 'NULL'], np.nan)\ndf_clean = df.dropna()\n```\n\n## Fixing Decimal Format Issues\n\n### European Decimal Notation (comma instead of period)\nIn some locales, commas are used as decimal separators (e.g., `12,34` means `12.34`).\n\n```python\ndef fix_decimal_comma(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert European decimal notation to standard.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(value, str):\n        # Replace comma with period for decimal\n        return value.replace(',', '.')\n    return value\n\n# Apply to specific columns or entire dataframe\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(fix_decimal_comma)\n\n# For multiple columns\nfor col in numeric_columns:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(fix_decimal_comma)\n```\n\n## Handling Scientific Notation\n\n### Converting Scientific Notation to Standard Format\n```python\ndef convert_scientific_to_float(value):\n    \"\"\"Convert scientific notation string to float.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(value, str):\n        try:\n            return float(value)\n        except ValueError:\n            return np.nan\n    return value\n\n# Apply conversion\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(convert_scientific_to_float)\n```\n\n### Formatting Output Without Scientific Notation\n```python\n# When saving, ensure no scientific notation\npd.set_option('display.float_format', '{:.2f}'.format)\n\n# Format specific columns\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(lambda x: f\"{x:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Converting String Values to Numeric\n\n```python\ndef clean_numeric_value(value):\n    \"\"\"\n    Clean a value that may have:\n    - Comma as decimal separator\n    - Scientific notation\n    - Other string issues\n    \"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(value):\n        return np.nan\n\n    if isinstance(value, (int, float)):\n        return float(value)\n\n    if isinstance(value, str):\n        # Replace comma with period\n        cleaned = value.replace(',', '.')\n        try:\n            return float(cleaned)\n        except ValueError:\n            return np.nan\n\n    return np.nan\n\n# Apply to all numeric columns\nnumeric_cols = df.columns[1:]  # Skip ID column\nfor col in numeric_cols:\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(clean_numeric_value)\n```\n\n## Rounding Values\n\n```python\n# Round to 2 decimal places\ndf = df.round(2)\n\n# Format as string with exactly 2 decimal places\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(lambda x: f\"{x:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Saving Clean Data\n\n```python\n# Save without scientific notation\ndf.to_csv('output.csv', index=False, float_format='%.2f')\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always make a copy** before modifying data\n2. **Check data types** before and after transformations\n3. **Validate transformations** on sample data first\n4. **Document all cleaning steps** for reproducibility\n5. **Handle edge cases** like empty strings, spaces, special characters\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":378,"cache_read_input_tokens":28499,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":378,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"87ac5997-0d9b-4524-8750-9741c28cef58","uuid":"28177450-9465-4d84-ae61-91192cf970c2"}

Factor |\n|------|---------------------|---------|-------------------|\n| Serum Creatinine | mg/dL | µmol/L | × 88.4 (to SI) or ÷ 88.4 (to US) |\n| BUN | mg/dL | mmol/L | × 0.357 (to SI) or ÷ 0.357 (to US) |\n| eGFR | mL/min/1.73m² | mL/min/1.73m² | Same unit |\n| Cystatin C | mg/L | mg/L | Same unit |\n| Uric Acid | mg/dL | µmol/L | × 59.48 (to SI) or ÷ 59.48 (to US) |\n\n**Physiological Ranges (US Conventional):**\n- Serum Creatinine: 0.5-1.5 mg/dL (normal), up to ~15-20 mg/dL in severe CKD\n- BUN: 7-25 mg/dL (normal), up to ~200 mg/dL in severe CKD\n- eGFR: >90 normal, 15-90 CKD stages, <15 ESRD\n- Cystatin C: 0.5-1.0 mg/L (normal), up to ~10 mg/L in CKD\n- Uric Acid: 2.5-8.0 mg/dL\n\n### Electrolytes\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Sodium | mEq/L (mmol/L) | 136-145 |\n| Potassium | mEq/L (mmol/L) | 3.5-5.5 |\n| Chloride | mEq/L (mmol/L) | 96-106 |\n| Bicarbonate | mEq/L (mmol/L) | 22-29 |\n| Magnesium | mg/dL | 1.5-2.5 |\n| Calcium (Total) | mg/dL | 8.5-10.5 |\n| Ionized Calcium | mmol/L | 1.1-1.35 |\n| Phosphorus | mg/dL | 2.5-4.5 |\n\n**Conversion for Magnesium:**\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: ÷ 2.43\n- If value is in mmol/L (>3.5), multiply by 2.43 to get mg/dL\n\n**Conversion for Calcium:**\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: ÷ 4.0\n- If value is in mmol/L (>3), multiply by 4.0 to get mg/dL\n\n**Conversion for Phosphorus:**\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: ÷ 3.1\n- If value is in mmol/L (>2), multiply by 3.1 to get mg/dL\n\n### Hematology\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Hemoglobin | g/dL | 12-17 |\n| Hematocrit | % | 36-52 |\n| RBC Count | million/µL | 4.0-6.0 |\n| WBC Count | thousand/µL | 4.0-11.0 |\n| Platelet Count | thousand/µL | 150-400 |\n\n**Hemoglobin Conversion:**\n- g/dL to g/L: × 10\n- If value is in g/L (>25), divide by 10 to get g/dL\n\n### Iron Studies\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Serum Iron | µg/dL | 60-170 |\n| TIBC | µg/dL | 250-400 |\n| Transferrin Saturation | % | 20-50 |\n| Ferritin | ng/mL | 20-300 (varies by sex) |\n\n**Serum Iron/TIBC Conversion:**\n- µg/dL to µmol/L: × 0.179\n- If value appears to be in µmol/L (much smaller), divide by 0.179\n\n### Proteins and Liver Function\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Albumin (Serum) | g/dL | 3.5-5.0 |\n| Total Protein | g/dL | 6.0-8.0 |\n| Total Bilirubin | mg/dL | 0.1-1.2 |\n| Direct Bilirubin | mg/dL | 0.0-0.4 |\n| Alkaline Phosphatase | U/L | 40-130 |\n| Prealbumin | mg/dL | 15-35 |\n\n**Albumin/Protein Conversion:**\n- g/dL to g/L: × 10\n- If value is in g/L (>10), divide by 10 to get g/dL\n\n**Bilirubin Conversion:**\n- mg/dL to µmol/L: × 17.1\n- If value is in µmol/L (>25), divide by 17.1 to get mg/dL\n\n### Lipids\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Total Cholesterol | mg/dL | <200 desirable |\n| LDL Cholesterol | mg/dL | <100 optimal |\n| HDL Cholesterol | mg/dL | >40 (men), >50 (women) |\n| Triglycerides | mg/dL | <150 |\n\n**Cholesterol multiply by 88.57 to get mg/dL\n\n### Glucose Metabolism\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Blood Glucose | mg/dL | 70-100 (fasting) |\n| HbA1c | % | <5.7 normal, >6.5 diabetes |\n| Fructosamine | µmol/L | 200-285 |\n\n**Glucose Conversion:**\n- mg/dL to mmol/L: ÷ 18.02\n- If value is in mmol/L (<40), multiply by 18.02 to get mg/dL\n\n### Hormones and Specialized Tests\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| PTH (Intact) | pg/mL | 15-65 |\n| Vitamin D (25-OH) | ng/mL | 30-100 |\n| Vitamin D (1,25-OH) | pg/mL | 20-80 |\n| Free T4 | ng/dL | 0.8-2.0 |\n| Free T3 | pg/mL | 2.3-4.2 |\n| TSH | mU/L | 0.4-4.0 |\n\n**Vitamin D (25-OH) Conversion:**\n- ng/mL to nmol/L: × 2.496\n- If value is in nmol/L (>100), divide by 2.496 to get ng/mL\n\n### Cardiac Markers\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| BNP | pg/mL | <100 |\n| NT-proBNP | pg/mL | <300 (varies by age) |\n| Troponin I | ng/mL | <0.04 |\n| Troponin T | ng/mL | <0.01 |\n\n### Blood Gases\n\n| Test | Unit | Range |\n|------|------|-------|\n| pH (Arterial) | pH units | 7.35-7.45 |\n| pCO2 | mmHg | 35-45 |\n| pO2 | mmHg | 80-100 |\n| Lactate | mmol/L | 0.5-2.0 |\n\n### Urine Tests\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| Urine Albumin | mg/L | <20 |\n| Urine Creatinine | mg/dL | varies |\n| UACR | mg/g | <30 normal |\n| UPCR | mg/g | <150 normal |\n| Urine pH | pH units | 4.5-8.0 |\n| Urine Specific Gravity | dimensionless | 1.005-1.030 |\n\n### Other Markers\n\n| Test | US Conventional Unit | Range |\n|------|---------------------|-------|\n| CRP | mg/L | <3.0 |\n| Beta-2 Microglobulin | mg/L | 0.8-2.4 |\n| Aluminum | µg/L | <10 |\n| Reticulocyte Count | % | 0.5-2.0 |\n\n## Python Implementation for Unit Detection and Conversion\n\n```python\n# Dictionary of lab parameters with their expected ranges and conversion info\nLAB_REFERENCE = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': {\n        'us_unit': 'mg/dL',\n        'us_range': (0.3, 20),  # Extended for CKD patients\n        'alt_multiplier': 88.4,  # SI value / this = US value\n    },\n    'BUN': {\n        'us_unit': 'mg/dL',\n        'us_range': (5, 200),  # Extended for CKD\n        'alt_multiplier': 0.357,\n    },\n    'Hemoglobin': {\n        'us_unit': 'g/dL',\n        'us_range': (5, 20),\n        'alt_multiplier': 10,  # g/L to g/dL\n    },\n    # ... etc\n}\n\ndef detect_and_convert(value, reference):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect if value is in alternative units and convert if needed.\n    \"\"\"\n    min_val, max_val = reference['us_range']\n    multiplier = reference.get('alt_multiplier', 1)\n\n    if value < min_val or value > max_val:\n        # Likely in alternative units, convert\n        converted = value / multiplier\n        if min_val <= converted <= max_val:\n            return converted\n    return value\n```\n\n## Key Principles\n\n1. **Identify outliers** - Values outside physiological ranges are likely in wrong units\n2. **Know the conversion factors** - Each lab parameter has specific conversion ratios\n3. **Consider clinical context** - CKD patients may have values outside \"normal\" ranges but still physiologically possible\n4. **Validate after conversion** - Ensure converted values fall within Skip ID column\n    df[col] = df[col].apply(fix_decimal)\n```\n\n### Scientific Notation in Input\n```python\n# Scientific notation strings are handled automatically by float()\nvalue = float('1.23e+02')  # Returns 123.0\n```\n\n### Preventing Scientific Notation in Output\n```python\n# Option 1: float_format in to_csv\ndf.to_csv('output.csv', float_format='%.2f')\n\n# Option 2: Pre-format as strings\ndf['column'] = df['column'].apply(lambda x: f\"{x:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Validation After Reading\n\n```python\n# Check for expected columns\nexpected_cols = ['col1', 'col2', 'col3']\nassert all(col in df.columns for col in expected_cols)\n\n# Check data types\nprint(df.dtypes)\n\n# Check for unexpected values\nprint(df.describe())\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Read as strings first** when dealing with mixed formats\n2. **Clean data in memory** before converting to numeric types\n3. **Use float_format** to control output precision\n4. **Always use index=False** unless index is meaningful data\n5. **Validate output** by reading back and checking a few rows\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2725,"cache_read_input_tokens":33027,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2725,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"87ac5997-0d9b-4524-8750-9741c28cef58","uuid":"bcbcb537-e611-4d29-b355-c9f589471aa8"}

Range-Based Validation\nThe most effective approach for detecting unit issues in lab data is range-based validation:\n\n```python\ndef validate_in_range(value, min_val, max_val):\n    \"\"\"Check if value falls within expected range.\"\"\"\n    return min_val <= value <= max_val\n```\n\n### 2. Outlier Detection for Unit Issues\n\nValues outside expected ranges often indicate:\n- Wrong unit (e.g., µmol/L instead of mg/dL)\n- Decimal placement error\n- Data entry error\n\n```python\ndef detect_unit_issue(value, expected_range, conversion_factor):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect if a value is likely in wrong units.\n\n    Args:\n        value: The numeric value to check\n        expected_range: (min, max) tuple of expected range\n        conversion_factor: Factor to convert from alternative unit\n\n    Returns:\n        'correct' if in range, 'needs_conversion' if convertible, 'invalid' otherwise\n    \"\"\"\n    min_val, max_val = expected_range\n\n    if min_val <= value <= max_val:\n        return 'correct'\n\n    # Try converting from alternative unit\n    converted = value / conversion_factor\n    if min_val <= converted <= max_val:\n        return 'needs_conversion'\n\n    # Try reverse conversion (in case factor is inverted)\n    converted_reverse = value * conversion_factor\n    if min_val <= converted_reverse <= max_val:\n        return 'needs_reverse_conversion'\n\n    return 'invalid'\n```\n\n## Implementation Pattern\n\n### Step 1: Define Reference Ranges and Conversions\n\n```python\nLAB_PARAMS = {\n    'Serum_Creatinine': {\n        'range': (0.3, 25),  # mg/dL\n        'conversion': 88.4,  # µmol/L to mg/dL: divide by this\n        'direction': 'divide',  # If out of range and too high, divide\n    },\n    'Hemoglobin': {\n        'range': (5, 22),  # g/dL\n        'conversion': 10,  # g/L to g/dL: divide by 10\n        'direction': 'divide',\n    },\n    'Blood_Glucose': {\n        'range': (40, 1000),  # mg/dL\n        'conversion': 18.02,  # mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by this\n        'direction': 'multiply',\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Apply Conversion Logic\n\n```python\ndef harmonize_value(value, param_config):\n    \"\"\"\n    Harmonize a value to the expected unit.\n\n    Args:\n        value: The numeric value\n        param_config: Dict with 'range', 'conversion', 'direction'\n\n    Returns:\n        Harmonized value in expected units\n    \"\"\"\n    min_val, max_val = param_config['range']\n    conv = param_config['conversion']\n    direction = param_config['direction']\n\n    # If already in range, return as-is\n    if min_val <= value <= max_val:\n        return value\n\n    # Try conversion\n    if direction == 'divide':\n        converted = value / conv\n    else:  # multiply\n        converted = value * conv\n\n    # If conversion brings into range, use it\n    if min_val <= converted <= max_val:\n        return converted\n\n    # Otherwise, try opposite direction\n    if direction == 'divide':\n        alt_converted = value * conv\n    else:\n        alt_converted = value / conv\n\n    if min_val <= alt_converted <= max_val:\n        return alt_converted\n\n    # If nothing works, return original (may need manual review)\n    return value\n```\n\n### Step 3: Apply to DataFrame\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndef harmonize_lab_data(df, lab_params):\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply harmonization to all columns.\n\n    Args:\n        df: DataFrame with lab data\n        lab_params: Dict of parameter configurations\n\n    Returns:\n        Harmonized DataFrame\n    \"\"\"\n    df_harmonized = df.copy()\n\n    for col, config in lab_params.items():\n        if col in df_harmonized.columns:\n            df_harmonized[col] = df_harmonized[col].apply(\n                lambda x: harmonize_value(x, config) if pd.notna(x) else x\n            )\n\n    return df_harmonized\n```\n\n## Validation Report\n\nGenerate a report showing what was converted:\n\n```python\ndef generate_validation_report(df_original, df_harmonized, lab_params):\n    \"\"\"Generate report of changes made.\"\"\"\n    report = []\n\n    for col in lab_params.keys():\n        if col in df_original.columns:\n            original = df_original[col]\n            harmonized = df_harmonized[col]\n\n            changed = (original != harmonized).sum()\n            if changed > 0:\n                report.append(f\"{col}: {changed} values converted\")\n\n    return '\\n'.join(report)\n```\n\n## Edge Cases\n\n1. **Values at boundary**: Use slightly extended ranges to avoid false positives\n2. **Multiple valid conversions**: Choose the one that produces most plausible result\n3. **Truly abnormal values**: Some CKD patients may have extreme values that are real\n4. **Zero values**: May indicate missing data rather than true zeros\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document all conversions** - Log which values were changed\n2. **Use clinical judgment** - Some extreme values may be real\n3. **Validate results** - Check that converted values are clinically plausible\n4. **Handle edge cases explicitly** - Don't let ambiguous cases pass silently\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":948,"cache_read_input_tokens":35752,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":948,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"87ac5997-0d9b-4524-8750-9741c28cef58","uuid":"05a57b19-eaa1-4a24-a2a5-db6af0181052"}

g/dL\n        'conversion': 10,  # g/L to g/dL: divide by 10\n        'direction': 'divide',\n    },\n    'Blood_Glucose': {\n        'range': (40, 1000),  # mg/dL\n        'conversion': 18.02,  # mmol/L to mg/dL: multiply by this\n        'direction': 'multiply',\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Apply Conversion Logic\n\n```python\ndef harmonize_value(value, param_config):\n    \"\"\"\n    Harmonize a value to the expected unit.\n\n    Args:\n        value: The numeric value\n        param_config: Dict with 'range', 'conversion', 'direction'\n\n    Returns:\n        Harmonized value in expected units\n    \"\"\"\n    min_val, max_val = param_config['range']\n    conv = param_config['conversion']\n    direction = param_config['direction']\n\n    # If already in range, return as-is\n    if min_val <= value <= max_val:\n        return value\n\n    # Try conversion\n    if direction == 'divide':\n        converted = value / conv\n    else:  # multiply\n        converted = value * conv\n\n    # If conversion brings into range, use it\n    if min_val <= converted <= max_val:\n        return converted\n\n    # Otherwise, try opposite direction\n    if direction == 'divide':\n        alt_converted = value * conv\n    else:\n        alt_converted = value / conv\n\n    if min_val <= alt_converted <= max_val:\n        return alt_converted\n\n    # If nothing works, return original (may need manual review)\n    return value\n```\n\n### Step 3: Apply to DataFrame\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndef harmonize_lab_data(df, lab_params):\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply harmonization to all columns.\n\n    Args:\n        df: DataFrame with lab data\n        lab_params: Dict of parameter configurations\n\n    Returns:\n        Harmonized DataFrame\n    \"\"\"\n    df_harmonized = df.copy()\n\n    for col, config in lab_params.items():\n        if col in df_harmonized.columns:\n            df_harmonized[col] = df_harmonized[col].apply(\n                lambda x: harmonize_value(x, config) if pd.notna(x) else x\n            )\n\n    return df_harmonized\n```\n\n## Validation Report\n\nGenerate a report showing what was converted:\n\n```python\ndef generate_validation_report(df_original, df_harmonized, lab_params):\n    \"\"\"Generate report of changes made.\"\"\"\n    report = []\n\n    for col in lab_params.keys():\n        if col in df_original.columns:\n            original = df_original[col]\n            harmonized = df_harmonized[col]\n\n            changed = (original != harmonized).sum()\n            if changed > 0:\n                report.append(f\"{col}: {changed} values converted\")\n\n    return '\\n'.join(report)\n```\n\n## Edge Cases\n\n1. **Values at boundary**: Use slightly extended ranges to avoid false positives\n2. **Multiple valid conversions**: Choose the one that produces most plausible result\n3. **Truly abnormal values**: Some CKD patients may have extreme values that are real\n4. **Zero values**: May indicate missing data rather than true zeros\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Document all conversions** - Log which values were changed\n2. **Use clinical judgment** - Some extreme values may be real\n3. **Validate results** - Check that converted values are clinically plausible\n4. **Handle edge cases explicitly** - Don't let ambiguous cases pass silently\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
